--
specs\storage-retention.md-2394-| CT-14 | A pending record with a changed device/inode, provider object generation, source hash, summary state, index state or output hash refuses and never rebuilds it. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2395-| CT-15 | Changed or newly created run content after cap measurement causes no candidate removal; any earlier compaction is disclosed separately. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2396-| CT-16 | Eligible run files are not partially removed when protected bytes would remain above 200 MB; the successful inverse case records removal before continuation. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2397:| CT-25 | Kill before/after exclusive empty-file creation and file fsync, provider object-generation/mount-discriminator and hash capture, `rotation-index.tmp` exclusive creation/write/fsync/identity proof, seal-temp fsync, progress rename/directory fsync, source rename, `log.next` promotion, index-temp promotion and index/directory fsync. A crash before durable progress preserves and refuses every orphan, including the precreated `log.next`. After progress, only both exact provider proofs and their enumerated journal/index name prefixes may resume and complete one rotation. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2398-| CT-26 | Crash at each retained-output boundary: a final file is admitted before committed-output progress only when its exact durable `outputOrigin` and bound temp identity already exist; progress-before-next-cursor resumes by committed offset. Linked, extra, partial, changed or originless temporary/final output is preserved and refused. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2399:| CT-39 | At every phase-`seal` and cancellation crash point, attempt a normal call append and an admitted `stop` result append. While `sealGate = preparing`, both either complete rotation and append to the new active `log`, or durably cancel, remove the exact admitted `log.next`, fsync, and append to the unchanged source. A crash after cancellation tombstone before next-file removal must resume removal; neither route ever appends to a preparation-gated source. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2400-| CT-40 | Kill before output-origin publication, after origin but before temp creation, after temp fsync before identity bind, after identity bind before rename, after rename, and after committed output progress. Recovery uses only the matching origin record and exact inode/bytes; an output with no origin, wrong range/hash/path/identity, or origin for another chunk is preserved and refused. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2401:| CT-27 | Schema-2 migration with valid, malformed or linked `compaction.pending.json` changes no marker, state, log, summary/index or pending bytes. A compatible schema-2 completion is the only path that removes it; explicit refusal and every speculative schema-3 interpretation leave the gate closed. It cannot manufacture a provider snapshot or bypass the P91 handoff. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2402:| CT-29 | At each `MigrationTransitionV3` crash point (clean P91 witness/source attestation; provider reserve/copy/file-copy sync/completion; `prepared`; durable phase `snapshot`; its summary/index/key prefix; `state.prev`; `state`; marker; the persisted `marker` evidence prefix; `migration-evidence-installed`; recovery temp/final; `remove`; terminal release; each evidence deletion; transition unlink/sync), a schema-2/schema-3 observer admits only the persisted prefix or refuses unchanged. The provider returns only bounded status, Tenet performs zero legacy-state hash/serialization/export calls, and provider copies remain intact. Field-mutated P91 witness, changed source generation and first-unit copy-set overflow fail before attestation. Reservation-time ENOSPC does likewise; copy-time ENOSPC leaves only `prepared` plus the same incomplete handle. Forward `prepared` cannot admit a target; `snapshot` cannot admit state/marker. Fresh starts at `prepared`; repair starts key-only. Restart twice; no cache, job or dependent effect crosses removal/release. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2403-| CT-36 | From phase `key` onward, restore either summary or index to its exact before pair, replace it with same bytes at a different inode or fresh provider generation while the old tuple is preserved, or use a third hash. Every case refuses unchanged; the exact after identity/content pair is the only admitted metadata state. Repair is the inverse control: every phase admits only its exact unchanged pair. `marker` and `state` rows remain exact as specified. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2404-| CT-37 | For both a sealed generation and a committed retained output, replace one middle UTF-8 byte with a different same-length byte while preserving inode and, for a generation, the first/last records. `JournalIndex` identity and size still match, but whole-file SHA-256 does not; recovery and compaction refuse before parsing result pairs or removing any file. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2405-| CT-38 | Replace a sealed generation or committed retained output with a regular one-link file of byte-identical length/content at a different inode, then with a replacement retaining device/inode/type/link values but a fresh provider generation. Whole-file SHA-256 matches; stable object identity does not. The file remains and recovery/compaction refuse. Same-object rename with unchanged generation remains exact. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2406-| CT-30 | Kill before and after every summary, rotation-index and retained-index replacement. Retry accepts only the durable before/anticipated-after identity-and-hash pair. Equal prefix hashes at another inode or at a fresh object generation with the same device/inode/type/link tuple, a third hash, malformed progress, a changed active log, unproved `rotation-index.tmp` and a new temp after destination promotion are refusal cases. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2407:| CT-31 | `log.next` and `rotation-index.tmp` origin controls: progress absent, wrong exclusive token or batch, symlink/reparse/case/textual alias, hard link, non-regular type, multiple links, changed root/directory identity, changed provider mount discriminator while root/directory/file tuples remain equal, changed object generation, provider unavailable, changed inode, wrong byte length/hash and each exact reservation are distinct. Only the exact sealed tuples and exact index promotion prefix proceed; every other case is preserved, and alias/mount/token ambiguity never falls back to path, bytes or inode equality. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2408:| CT-33 | Clean after tuples alter content/stable identity/provider generation/schema, phase, population version/order/hash or operation kind and refuse. Reordered JSON keys reproduce the fixed `afterSnapshotHash`/`populationHash` vectors; reordered members/targets and changed domain/version do not. Forward legacy controls ask the provider about `state.prev`, `state` and `log`: same inode plus one changed byte, different inode plus equal bytes, fresh provider object generation with the old tuple preserved, changed P91 witness/generation, copied/foreign/missing/partial handle and unavailable provider all refuse without exposing bytes or a fingerprint. Instrumentation proves zero Tenet hash/serialize/export calls on legacy source bytes. A separate malformed/ID-changed key control never prints key bytes. Only exact clean after tuples plus provider `exact` and the persisted prefix resume. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2409-| CT-55 | Kill twice before/after private reserve, missing-attestation orphan readmit, terminal release and lost acknowledgment. Exact reserve/readmit yields one handle and one private capacity reservation; changed request/source yields conflict/mismatch. `released` and replayed `already-released` are the only states admitting the next evidence-deletion prefix. `accepted.json`, provisional/copied postcondition, compaction, `cleanTemp`, unrelated operation and unconfirmed forget cannot release or delete evidence. Every unauthorized path preserves both provider set and evidence byte/effect idempotently. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2410:| CT-56 | Fail exclusive creation, file fsync, identity/hash capture, temp proof cross-binding, progress-temp publication, seal fsync, re-admission and each unlink/fsync cleanup boundary. Before seal publication starts, the live holder cleans only both exact still-open reservations, as one exact pair, or reports uncertainty; failure proving `rotation-index.tmp` preserves the precreated `log.next`. After publication starts, no failure deletes either possibly sealed leaf. Restart distinguishes absent-final/temp-only from exact final, re-syncs/admits only the exact final prefix and preserves/refuses orphan or temp-only pairs rather than recreating, adopting or generically cleaning them. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2411:| CT-57 | `rotation-index.tmp` proof/admission and replay: mutate every authorization leaf and each duplicated enclosing field; omit/extra/future one; copy the proof from another run, batch, generation, operation token or progress phase; alter either reservation, index before/after hash, source/sealed/destination or promotion phase; use a same-byte different-inode temp, same-inode changed bytes, same-path/same-bytes/device/inode/type/link replacement with a fresh object generation, alias/reparse/hardlink/cross-mount/provider-unavailable/wrong-token leaf; present a temp with no progress/proof, a proof with missing temp, an after-destination with a missing proof, and an after-destination plus a new temp (already-replaced); then kill/restart twice before and after index promotion. Also replay after changing only the provider mount discriminator while the root, `.tenet` directory and file tuples and tokens are preserved. Only the exact pre-promotion pair or exact post-promotion pair completes. Every other case preserves `log.next`, `rotation-index.tmp` and destination bytes, serializes only `storage-accounting-unknown`, performs no object/inode adoption, cleanup or progress advance, and exposes no path/provider token/root/inode/object-generation/hash in the reason or response fields. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2412:| CT-58 | For every persisted `StableIdentity` role used by compaction, migration evidence and cleanup, replace the admitted object at the same path and with byte-identical content after provider capture while preserving device, inode, type and link count. Kill before publication and after the proof is durable, restart twice and require provider-generation mismatch, byte preservation and no publication/progress advance. Repeat with the same bytes and identity after a same-object no-replace rename: the durable generation remains valid and resumes. Reusing, inventing or accepting a caller-supplied generation, or silently falling back to the old inode tuple, is a failure. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2413:| CT-59 | For each exact reservation, capture a provider mount binding, kill, and replay after changing the mounted-directory discriminator while preserving the marker root, `.tenet` directory identity, file path/bytes/`StableIdentity`, token namespace and exclusive token. The provider returns mismatch/unavailable as appropriate; restart twice performs no cleanup, rename, progress advance or inode adoption and serializes only `storage-accounting-unknown`. The unchanged-mount control resumes only the exact sealed prefix. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2414-| CT-34 | Kill before `finalState`, before retained-index publication, after index publication, after source deletion and before pending removal. Replace only `state` or only `state.prev` with equal bytes or changed bytes, including an equal-byte replacement with the same old tuple but a fresh provider generation. Every identity/content mismatch preserves source/progress and refuses; the exact original pair reaches byte-stable completion. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2415-| CT-41 | Kill after source removal but before response publication, and after state/call publication but before progress removal. The first restart publishes one historical operation report; the second removes progress without another count, deletion or report effect. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2416-| CT-42 | Replace only the active `log` with byte-identical content at a different stable identity, including a fresh provider generation while device/inode/type/link remain equal. The index/content read still matches journal bytes, but compaction and dependent mutation refuse as `changed` before appending. |

specs\storage-retention.md-2500-| Risk | Mitigation and acceptance evidence |
specs\storage-retention.md-2501-|---|---|
specs\storage-retention.md-2502-| Index or summary corruption could make complete journal history appear empty. | Strict hashes and phase pairs; no fallthrough to active `log`; CT-13 and CT-14. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2503:| `log.next` could be mistaken for the active log after an identity crash. | The provider reserves an empty file first, then progress seals its exclusive-name token, exact root/directory/token-namespace mount binding, stable object generation and empty hash before any rename. Recovery never creates or adopts an unsealed empty file; CT-25, CT-31, CT-56, CT-58 and CT-59. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2504:| `rotation-index.tmp` could be replaced before index promotion, then adopted later by path or hash. | The provider proves and seals the temp inode before progress, bound to the exact rotation authorization and both index hashes. Recovery admits only its exact before- or after-promotion prefix, preserves unproved/already-replaced cases and serializes only `storage-accounting-unknown`; CT-25, CT-30, CT-31, CT-56 and CT-57. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2505-| A retained-output crash could duplicate bytes or lose protected records. | Exact deterministic chunk names, source range, byte/hash admission and an atomic cursor/output-list replacement; CT-26. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2506:| Two kernel versions could publish across a schema migration. | `migration-transition.json` is a durable cross-version fence; marker changes last, and only exact before/after states are admitted; CT-29. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2507-| A summary or index could be partly replaced without a durable proof of which batch owns it. | Progress stores the complete anticipated after hash before each target write; CT-30. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2508-| A crash could count a batch twice. | One pending batch ID, before/after hashes and exactly one additive summary transition; CT-8 to CT-10. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2509-| Distinct byte-identical batches could share an input identity. | Random generation and batch IDs; source hashes prove bytes only; CT-10. |
--
specs\storage-retention.md-2519-| Ordinary recovery could append while a summary is pending. | Append only to the active generation after sealing; P99 remains independent of the source hash; CT-9. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2520-| `stop` could be blocked by cleanup. | Stop may skip new reduction, after reserving its durable response, and resumes pending work later; CT-19. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2521-| Reserve accounting could omit retained output or double-count state. | Exact identity-based byte projection includes every old, temporary and new leaf before the first write; CT-16 and CT-19. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2522:| Missing state could be treated as fresh by an empty index. | P91 history classification runs before index migration or reduction; CT-17 and CT-18. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2523-| Legacy summary totals may already be wrong. | Preserve strict valid v1 counts only with an explicit historical-aggregate disclosure; exact-dependent work refuses without the exact §16.9.1 retention or proved-count migration, and no silent reset occurs. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2524-| A schema-3 migration could reinterpret the old hash-only pending compaction. | Migration refuses every old pending record before writing schema-3 metadata; CT-27. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2525:| A migration could prove clean new bytes but admit substituted legacy source content. | Generated targets have exact clean after tuples; legacy state/log source uses provider exact comparison and opaque private copies. No aggregate legacy byte hash exists; CT-33. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2526-| State could change while a publisher proof authorizes deletion. | Progress binds and repeatedly rechecks both state identities and content hashes before index replacement, source deletion and final cleanup; CT-34. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2527-| A current publisher guard could omit side fields while its primary marker looks applied. | Effect rows enumerate every state field changed by the current publisher and CT-32 mutates each independently. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2528-| Publisherless protected pairs could fill the store. | Retain those pairs forever, do not reinterpret a completed result, and return `storage-protected-full`; CT-47 proves the no-deletion boundary. |

specs\storage-retention.md-2535-

specs\storage-retention.md-2589-| Age/cap deletion had no per-operation crash barrier, so a kill could leave an unreported partial prefix. | Closed locally with one `cleanup.retention.json`, complete preflight, ordered cursor and exact present/absent recovery. specs\storage-retention.md:2590:| Migration step 7 attempted to advance to `marker` twice. | Corrected locally: phase `marker` and `afterSnapshotHash` are one transition; `remove` is subsequent. |
specs\storage-retention.md-2591-| The signed continuation decision required the exact original verb/projection after restart, but `RetentionProgress` had no durable action and CT-46 did not test reconstruction or cross-call mismatch. | **Closed by owner-signed amendment.** The record now has strict immutable `originalAction`, reuses schema-3 `mutating-call-v1`, binds the journal intent token, reserved run and enclosing batch/generation, carries no arbitrary bytes and refuses malformed/mismatched/unknown recovery before report. CT-46 now has explicit red/green, crash, restart, later-mutator and mismatch controls. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2592:| The former first-rotation order authorized `log.next` in progress before the file existed, then attempted token creation, token-to-empty truncation and later identity adoption. A crash could leave an unbound name, and an empty hash at that name did not prove the provider reservation or its mount/root identity. | **Closed by owner-selected amendment.** The provider first exclusively reserves a private empty `log.next`, fsyncs it and returns the exact mounted-file identity/hash. Phase-`seal` progress then journals the complete reservation, before any source rename or handler effect. The exact inode is promoted by atomic no-replace rename. Pre-seal live failure cleans only the still-held exact reservation; crash before seal preserves/refuses the orphan; sealed restart follows only the recorded name/identity/hash/mount prefix. CT-25, CT-31 and CT-56 carry failure, cleanup, alias, mount, crash and refusal controls. No retention, migration or P87 owner policy changes. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2593:| The safe reservation-first order proved `log.next` but did not persist provider identity for the precreated `rotation-index.tmp`. A later same-path file could be mistaken for the prepared after-index inode, and a provider/path/hash observation alone could not bind it to the run, batch, action, reservation, source/destination or promotion phase. | **Closed by owner decision.** Phase-`seal` progress now persists `RotationIndexTempProofV1`: a distinct provider reservation bound to the exact operation/run, batch/generation, progress phase, original-action/token, complete `EmptyRotationReservationV1`, log source/sealed/destination and exact index before/after proof. Recovery admits only the exact temp-before/destination-before or temp-absent/destination-after prefix. Missing/unproved, copied, cross-bound and already-replaced temp states are preserved and refused; no inode is adopted. A temp-proof failure never deletes a precreated `log.next`. Alias/mount/token/provider failure and serialization use only `storage-accounting-unknown`, with no path/identity proof disclosed. CT-25/30/31/56/57 own crash, replay and cleanup. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2594:| The rotation records claimed a provider-proved mount relation but persisted no mount discriminator. A different mounted directory could preserve every serialized root/directory/file tuple and replay as exact. | **Closed locally, mechanism only.** `ReservationMountBindingV1` persists the marker root, `.tenet` stable directory identity, fixed token namespace and provider-verifiable opaque discriminator. Provider re-verification is mandatory; changed-mount replay refuses even when the previous tuples and token still compare equal. CT-31, CT-57 and CT-59 own changed-mount proof and two restarts. |
specs\storage-retention.md:2595:| `StableIdentity` used device/inode/type/link and hash but no provider generation. An object replacement could reuse all of those tuple values, including an equal-byte leaf, and still be adopted. | **Closed locally, mechanism only.** `StableIdentity` and the coupled `SnapshotFileIdentityV1` require a durable provider-issued non-reusable generation. Tenet never derives/decodes the provider-opaque value and has no arbitrary-inode fallback. Provider unavailability and same-tuple replacement refuse; same-object rename preserves identity. CT-30, CT-31, CT-38, CT-42, CT-45, CT-57 and CT-58 cover all persisted roles and crash/restart replacement. |

specs\marker-identity-continuity.md-2436-
specs\marker-identity-continuity.md:2437:The segmented-storage draft previously authorized `log.next` in progress
specs\marker-identity-continuity.md-2438-before it had a provider-backed object, then created token bytes and truncated
specs\marker-identity-continuity.md-2439-the file while treating a later inode observation as provenance. That leaves a
specs\marker-identity-continuity.md-2440-crash path with no exact exclusive-name, alias or mount identity and lets an
specs\marker-identity-continuity.md-2441-arbitrary pre-existing empty file look like a delayed creation. The
specs\marker-identity-continuity.md-2442-owner-selected disposition is reservation first: the provider creates and
specs\marker-identity-continuity.md:2443:fsyncs an empty private `log.next`, captures the provider-verifiable
specs\marker-identity-continuity.md-2444-root/directory/token-namespace mount binding, durable object generation and
specs\marker-identity-continuity.md-2445-exact file identity and empty hash, then phase-`seal` progress journals that
specs\marker-identity-continuity.md-2446-complete tuple. Only the sealed exact inode is promoted.

specs\plan.md-360-10. **Result replay for a known effect without a publisher:** clarify Tech Spec §§7.4 and 15.1 without weakening never-waivable AC-17; dependency resolution is the concrete case (§12.3). **Decided 2026-11-17 (owner): option A.** The general rule (§7.4 line 910) governs every known effect. "A completed record without a publisher has no state replay" applies only to unknown effect names (the inherited-property guard). A known effect whose result changes state, such as dependency resolution, must have a declared publisher and enough durable facts to replay its result idempotently. When those facts are missing, as in legacy records, recovery reports uncertainty and never silently drops the result. The Tech Spec §7.4 wording is clarified in the decision-9 amendment batch. Direction only. Still required: review and sign-off of the wording and of a dependency result, preview, legacy and interruption contract, then failing-first real-store tests.
specs\plan.md:361:11. **Marker identity and legacy records (P108/P109):** resolve how PRD §5.10.4's complete directory identity and conditional host-held key are implemented, and how existing markers are handled without trusting an unauthenticated copy. The unsigned [design draft](marker-identity-continuity.md) recommends investigating lossless filesystem identities, conditional host-key binding and preservation or refusal when legacy ownership cannot be established; an alternative is to refuse operation on affected platforms or records until the required identity can be established. Neither option waives the PRD; review same-filesystem moves, quarantine and continuity before approving a contract or spec amendment. **Decided 2026-11-17 (owner): option A.** Markers store lossless identities (bigint device and inode as decimal strings, plus a creation time verified per platform), bound to a host-held key when verified host storage exists. Legacy numeric markers are never upgraded automatically: they are kept where ownership can still be shown and refused with an honest reason where it cannot. The full contract (threat model, key lifecycle, quarantine, old/new-binary protocol) is written first, then re-reviewed and signed off before any code. The host-key part needs a separately authorized host-storage spike; the Tech Spec §7.5 amendment joins the decision-9 batch. Direction only; review blockers B1–B4 (AC-16 prerequisite now met) remain open.

specs\plan.md:1039:| 2026-11-19 UTC | **The corrected first-rotation protocol proved `log.next` but not the precreated `rotation-index.tmp` inode it later promoted.** A replacement temp at that path, or a destination already replaced before the exact provider proof was durable, could be admitted by path/hash after restart. No record bound the temp to the exact rotation authorization, reservation, run/batch/progress, action, source/destination or promotion phase. | Provider identity was represented for the journal reservation but omitted from the index-promotion source. The two files were ordered under one rotation without one strict cross-bound temp proof; a temp lookup therefore had no durable object authority and cleanup could risk a valid precreated `log.next`. | **Owner-signed storage/P87 mechanism amendment:** persist `RotationIndexTempProofV1` at phase `seal` with a distinct provider token and exact root/mount/file identity, after-index bytes/hash and complete cross-binding to `EmptyRotationReservationV1`, operation/run/batch/generation, original-action hash, progress phase, log source/sealed/destination, index-before and index-after, and fixed promotion phase. Admit only exact temp-before/index-before or absent-temp/index-after-at-captured-inode prefixes. Preserve/refuse unproved or already-replaced temp states; never adopt, create or retroactively bind an inode. specs\plan.md:2365:**P91/P106 missing-state run evidence and options (2026-11-16 UTC; source trace and simulated transaction, unsigned).** Commit, integration and dependency jobs currently use the same job/effect ID in `run-<id>` and their effect intent, but do not store the path in that intent. The current undo producer stores its run path in the intent; an older producer did not, and its pathless `inCall` is fenced when retained. A synthetic no-in-call recovery reaches the Git adapter's temp fallback (§13). Check jobs have a run ID only in `session.inCall`; scaffold writes have a separate `s-...` effect ID and their later install uses a different run ID. Accounting checkpoints retain in-flight work IDs, not the run ID, process identity or path. The current journal admits nonempty string IDs without a producer-specific path grammar, and `tempDir` creates directories and changes permissions. Therefore a presumed path derived from arbitrary journal IDs, or a call to `tempDir` during inspection, is not a reviewed ownership proof or a read-only lookup. A completed scaffold write is not evidence that its install started, ended or had no external effects.
